- 1 - 
個股報告 
巧新(1563) 
再生鋁與半導體雙引擎推升獲利 
投資評等 目標價 
逢低買進 $90 
前次投資建議：2026.08.1 
「買進」 ，目標價80

報告日期：2026.11.6 研 究 員：陳泂暉

摘 要 及 調 整 原 因 
➢ 受旺季與高端車款拉貨帶動，預估輪圈出貨 2026 年逾 60萬顆、2027 年達 70萬顆，營益率
目標回升至 20%。屏東新熔煉廠預計 27Q2 試產、27Q4 稼動率上看 70%；半導體零組件已
獲美系大廠認證，27Q2 起大批量放量出貨。惟近期市場波動較大，下調投資評等至「逢低買
進」，6個月目標價 90 (15X2027(F)PER)，潛在上漲空間 27.66%。

基本資料 結論與建議 
2026/11/5 收盤價： 70.50 ➢ 公司 26Q3營收 20.16 億元，QoQ+1.8%、YoY+18.5%，
營收較上季增加主要是因為下游客戶拉貨與輪圈出貨穩
健；毛利率 25.9%，QoQ+1.6ppts、YoY+8.3ppts，因車
用再生鋁使用比率顯著提升至 70%與產品優化；稅後淨
利 2.47 億元，QoQ+247.6%、YoY+478.1%，因 26Q3
日幣升值，對業外匯兌利益呈現正面貢獻；稅後EPS 1.48
元。 
➢ 受新車旺季與高端車款拉貨帶動，預估輪圈出貨 2026 年
逾 60 萬顆（下半年優於上半年） ，2027 年達 70 萬顆。
公司目標營業利益率回升至 20%。 
➢ 公司屏東新熔煉廠預計 27Q2 試產、27Q4 稼動率上看
70%；半導體零組件已獲美系大廠原生鋁認證並小批量出
貨，預計 27Q2 隨新廠試產邁入大批量放量出貨。 
➢ 預估 2027年營收 88.93億元 ，YoY+13.2%； 毛利率28%，
YoY+3ppts；稅後淨利9.99 億元，YoY+53.1%；稅後EPS 
5.9元。

巧新目前股價位於 12X27(F)PER，考量到再生鋁使用比重
提高以及半導體產品放量，惟近期市場波動較大，因此 投
資評等從「買進」下調至「逢低買進」 ，6 個月目標價
90(15X2027(F)PER)，潛在上漲空間 27.66%。

營 運 分 析 
                   巧新為國內汽車鍛造輪圈大廠 
巧新成立於 1994 年，為國內汽車鍛造輪圈大廠，主要產品包含鍛造鋁圈、
鋁材、其他(主要為鍛造鋁懸吊系統元件 )。公司目前有三座生產基地，分別
是雲林場、屏東廠及德國廠，鍛造輪圈總年產能為90萬件(雲林廠 60 萬件、
屏東廠 30萬件)。

公司客戶包括全球知名車廠，Ferrari(法拉利)、Porsche(保時捷)、Mclaren(麥
拉倫)、Bently(賓利)、Aston Martin、賓士(含 AMG)、BMW(含 Rolls-Royce、
Mini)、Jaguar & Land Rover、Lexus、Lotus及電動卡車製造商 Workhouse
等。

圖一、公司 2026 年下半年預估產品營收比重 
公司 2026 年下半年預估產品營收比重

資料來源：巧新法說會；兆豐投顧整理

公司 26Q3營收 20.16 億元，QoQ+1.8%、YoY+18.5%，營收較上季增加主
要是因為下游客戶拉貨與輪圈出貨穩健 ；毛利率 25.9%，QoQ+1.6ppts、
YoY+8.3ppts，因車用再生鋁使用比率顯著提升至 70%與產品優化；稅後淨
車用再生鋁採用率達
七成助攻毛利率

操作（預期未來六個月股價漲跌幅介於-15%與+15%之間）； 逢高賣出（預期未來六個月股價跌幅介於-15%與-30%之間）；賣出

- 3 - 
個股報告 
利 2.47 億元，QoQ+247.6%、YoY+478.1%，因 26Q3 日幣升值，對業外匯
兌利益呈現正面貢獻；稅後 EPS 1.48 元。

海運費回降與再生鋁推升 26Q4毛利創高 
展望 26H2，鍛造輪圈第三季出貨量為 15 萬顆，第四季因歐美車廠 12 月進
入聖誕與新年假期、客戶端主力車型下線及部分訂單提前於 9 月拉貨影響，
出貨動能略為放緩，因此預計 2026 全年輪圈出貨量約在 60 萬顆左右。而
2027 年則是持續受惠於車廠既有需求與新產品陸續銜接，預估2027 年全年
輪圈出貨量將接近 70萬顆。

在毛利與成本表現方面，第三季受貨櫃海運費較上半年暴增 50%至 150%影
響，推升營業費用並侵蝕營業利益；惟 10 月份起海運費已回落至相對合理
水準，第四季營業費用率將略降，且 2027 年隨產品組合擴大與短鏈供應，
費用率將進一步降低。獲利優化之核心動能持續來自再生鋁材的導入，26H2
再生鋁實際使用率已拉升並超過 70%（第四季維持約 70%水準） ，帶動第三
季毛利率達 26%，在匯率穩定前提下第四季毛利率有望挑戰歷史新高 28%；
儘管 2027 年再生鋁採用率預期回調至 50%至 60%之常態水位，但在毛利率
40%至 50%之半導體產品放量及產品組合調整挹注下，2027 年整體毛利率
表現預期將優於 2H26。

新熔煉廠 27Q2 試產迎大批量放量 
公司持續拓展鋁材多元應用，目前於屏東設立的熔煉廠正進行試量產，第二
座新熔煉廠預計於 27Q2 啟動試量產，受惠在手客戶需求強勁，稼動率爬坡
迅速，預估 27Q4 稼動率將提升至 70%以上。在半導體材料領域，公司持續
推進美系設備大廠之薄膜沉積與蝕機台設備腔體與關鍵零件之送樣與驗證作
業，目前已正式取得原生鋁認證（再生鋁認證與 AMS 規範持續推進中），
26Q3 及 26Q4 已啟動小批量出貨貢獻營收，並預計於 27Q2 新熔煉廠試量
產後邁入大批量放量出貨。

半導體放量與再生鋁驅動獲利體質升級 
預估 26Q4營收 19.86 億元 ，QoQ-1.5%、YoY+20.9%， 季減主因歐美車廠 12 
月進入聖誕與新年長假、客戶端一款主力車款產品下線退場以及部分客戶配
合年終盤點提前於 9 月拉貨；毛利率27.6%，QoQ+1.7ppts、YoY+9.3ppts，
受惠於再生鋁採用率上升；稅後淨利 2.03 億元，QoQ-17.8%、較去年同期
虧轉盈；稅後 EPS 1.2元。

預估 2026 年營收 78.54 億元，YoY+13.3%；毛利率 25%，YoY+3.7ppts；
稅後淨利 6.53億元，YoY+105.1%；稅後 EPS 3.86 元。

推升 26Q4毛利創高 
半導體放量與再生鋁
驅動獲利體質升級

## Document 037: 20261019_投資週報_通膨升溫增添波動，台積財報展現韌性

Source PDF: all report pdf/20261019_投資週報_通膨升溫增添波動，台積財報展現韌性.pdf
Report source: Yuanta (投資週報)
Date: 2026-10-19
Pages: 1
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同

金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 10 月 19 日
上週重點訊息
◼ 美國 9 月通膨升溫，Fed 年底前再升息機率仍高： 美國 9 月 CPI 年增 3.7%，高於 8 月的
3.4%；核心 CPI 年增率亦由 2.4%升至 2.6%，顯示通膨壓力不只來自能源，核心價格亦有回
升。隔日的 PPI 年增率則由 5.6%升至 6.3%，反映上游成本壓力仍強。通膨重新升溫，使Fed
即使面對就業降溫，也難以快速轉向寬鬆，市場對年底前再度升息的預期仍高。
◼ 台積電財報創新高，AI 需求強勁但市場轉向檢視融資風險：台積電 10/15 公布其第三季淨利

達 7,631 億元、年增 69%，連續第六季刷新紀錄，第三季美元營收 467.2 億美元，並預估第
四季營收將升至 523～543 億美元 ； 公司同時將2026 全年營收成長預估提高至約 44%， 反映
AI 晶片與先進製程需求仍極為強勁。然而，高利率環境使市場開始檢視 AI 資料中心業者的槓
桿與融資能力，CoreWeave 約 500 億美元債務引發信用疑慮，引發市場波動。
◼ 中東衝突再度推高能源風險 ， 油價高檔加劇全球通膨壓力 ：胡塞武裝上週攻擊沙烏地阿美設施 ，
伊朗亦表示採取新的海上攻擊策略，使荷莫茲海峽與波斯灣航運風險再度升高。10/16 布蘭特
原油與西德洲原油期貨雙雙收在近三週高點。
市場展望
◼ 科技股中期趨勢料將不變 ， 但短線波動勢將加劇 ：近期市場同時面臨通膨升溫 、AI 資本支出疑
慮與重要科技財報公佈在即等不確定因素，短線風險偏好可能受到壓抑。在利率維持高檔及市
場評價偏高的環境下，市場短線料將隨財報結果與市場預期而出現較大波動。然而，上週台積
電與 ASML 財報結果整體優於預期 ， 可見AI 相關企業營運與資本支出尚未出現全面轉弱跡象 ，

科技產業基本面仍具一定支撐，惟後續宜更加重視企業獲利、現金流及資本支出變動及科技子
產業的表現分化現象。
◼ 中天期美債與投資級債債券兼顧息收與利率風險：美國經濟仍具韌性 ， 加上政府與企業發債供
給維持高檔，可能使市場利率持續處於較高水準。雖然投資級債信用利差位於相對低檔，但企
業獲利、現金流及償債能力仍具支撐，加上較高的美國公債基準利率，使投資級債的利息收益
仍具吸引力。相較之下，短天期債券面臨未來利率下降後的再投資風險，長天期債券則較容易
受到公債供給增加 、 期限溢酬及利率波動影響 ， 因此中天期美國公債與投資債券較能兼顧息收
與利率風險。

AI 資本支出侵蝕現金流，科技巨頭財報拖累科技股 
                                   2026 年 10 月 30 日 
一、 市場動態 
1. AI 資本支出與現金流疑慮再起，那斯達克領跌：美股 10 月 29 日主要指數收黑，S&P 
500 下跌 0.30%、Nasdaq 下跌 1.38%、 道瓊下跌0.25%、 費半下跌1.42%， 費半ETF
盤後跌幅進一步擴大至逾 3%。 市場下跌主要因Alphabet 財報營收與獲利低於預期、
搜尋廣告業務成長放緩，加上大幅增加資本支出導致單季自由現金流轉負，股價重挫
12.64%，再度引發市場對 AI 投資報酬的疑慮，賣壓亦擴及半導體及軟體類股。其他
盤後公布財報的科技公司表現亦出現雜音，Amazon 資本支出明顯高於預期，近 12
個月自由現金流轉為淨流出，Intel 則因 PC 需求疲弱下修財測，使科技股盤後賣壓進
一步擴大。不過，當日 S&P 500 約 81%成分股上漲，顯示市場賣壓主要集中於 AI 相
關大型科技股，而非整體市場同步走弱。 
2. Fed 按兵不動但兩票主張升息，12 月升息機率仍高：Fed 於 10 月 28 日以 10 比 2 維
持利率於 3.75%～4.00%，Cook 與 Hammack 主張升息 1 碼，期貨市場隱含 12 月
升息機率約 85%。9 月 PCE 年增 3.6%，10 年期美債殖利率在 5%附近震盪。 
3. 中東情勢緊張 ， 油價維持百元以上高檔 ：布蘭特原油 10 月 28 日升至每桶 115 美元附
近，WTI 亦站上 102 美元。高油價增加通膨與 Fed 升息壓力，也推升 AI 資料中心電
力成本，持續壓抑科技股評價。

二、 大型科技企業財報重點： 
1. 雲端業務全面加速，AI 需求仍未降溫：本週公布財報的大型科技企業中，雲端業務成
長普遍加速。Alphabet 旗下 Google Cloud 營收 307.09 億美元、年增 103%，TPU
系統銷售成為重要動能；Microsoft Azure 年增 48%，下季財測進一步加速至 49～
50%；Amazon AWS 營收 459.8 億美元、年增 39.3%，為近五年最快增速。Intel 資
料中心與 AI 營收亦年增 78%，創 15 年來最強成長。各家均表示算力需求仍高於可用
供給，顯示 AI 基礎建設需求尚未出現降溫跡象。 
2. 資本支出持續攀升，自由現金流表現決定股價分歧：Microsoft 於 10 月 28 日盤後公
布 2027 會計年度第一季財報，營收 922.79 億美元、年增 18.8%，營業利益率達
49.8%，自由現金流亦維持正值，整體財報優於市場預期。相較之下，Alphabet 雲端
業務表現亮眼，但廣告業務成長未達預期，且單季資本支出達 513.55 億美元，導致
自由現金流轉為負 90.65 億美元，股價於 10 月 29 日重挫 12.64%；Amazon 單季資
本支出則達 773 億美元，近 12 個月自由現金流轉為淨流出 424 億美元，加上第四季
營收財測中值低於預期 ， 盤後股價下跌逾6%。 資本支出指引方面 ，Alphabet 維持 2026
年 1,950 億至 2,050 億美元，Amazon 維持約 2,200 億美元，Microsoft 則因第三方

預期約 171 億美元；PC 疲弱延續至 1H27
Oracle
FY30 營收目標逾 2,250 億美元；FY29 自由現金流逾 300
億美元；額外籌資上限 200 億美元（股債各 100 億美元）
自由現金流於 FY28 下半年轉正；FY29 淨
負債／調整後 EBITDA 低於 3.5 倍
資料來源：財報新聞稿，元大銀行整理
三、 市場看法：
1. 市場不再單純獎勵 AI 投資，開始要求可驗證的財務報酬：Microsoft 與 Alphabet 同

樣大舉投資 AI， 股價反應卻明顯分歧 ， 關鍵在於AI 投資是否已轉化為獲利與現金流。
我們認為，市場評價標準已由「是否投資AI」轉向「AI 投資能否帶來足夠的營收成長
與現金流」 ， 具備穩定現金流與明確商業化能力的企業將相對受青睞 ， 而自由現金流持
續為負、高度依賴外部融資的業者則可能面臨評價壓縮。

不另行通知。
2. 雲端未認列合約持續擴大 ，AI 需求尚未出現結構性反轉 ：Microsoft 商業 RPO 達 6,770
億美元、年增 73%，Oracle RPO 約 6,640 億美元，AWS 與 Google Cloud backlog
亦分別達 5,780 億及 5,610 億美元，四家合計約 2.48 兆美元（詳見圖 3） 。這顯示目
前市場疑慮主要在於投資回收期與資金壓力，而非終端需求惡化；只要雲端業者持續
擴充算力，先進製程、伺服器與資料中心供應鏈中期仍具支撐。
3. 資本支出 2027 年仍將成長，台灣 AI 供應鏈持續受惠：四大雲端／平台業者 2026 年

資本支出合計約 7,200～7,400 億美元 ， 且2027 年仍將增加 。 元大投顧認為 ，Google
擴大 TPU 對外銷售及 Microsoft 持續部署 GPU 與自研 Maia 加速器，將帶動伺服器
ODM、 散熱及機殼需求 ， 受惠供應鏈包括廣達 、 奇鋐 、 雙鴻 、 健策 、 富世達及聯發科。
融資面則宜留意 AI 相關企業公司債利差及再融資條件變化。
4. Fed 鷹派立場限制評價空間，短線震盪可能延續：12 月升息預期升溫、通膨與油價維
持高檔，使 10 年期美債殖利率停留在 5%附近，壓抑高評價科技股評價擴張。只要
AI 需求未出現結構性反轉，維持短線審慎，對科技股中期偏正向看法。
圖 3：主要雲端業者未認列合約（Backlog／RPO）規模（單位：億美元）

四、市場展望：
1. 科技股短線波動較大，中期看好現金流穩健的 AI 受惠企業：本週財報顯示雲端營收加速
成長與未認列合約持續擴大，AI 需求仍強；但資本支出攀升、自由現金流轉弱，加上Fed
可能於 12 月升息，短線科技股波動恐將延續。中期而言，AI 產業將由全面成長轉向基本
面分化，具技術優勢、財務結構穩健且 AI 投資已能轉化為獲利的企業，如雲端與 AI 軟體
平台領先者，以及先進製程、伺服器、散熱等關鍵供應鏈，風險報酬相對有利；對高度依
賴外部融資、自由現金流持續為負的企業則宜維持審慎，並留意零組件成本上升對 PC 等
消費性電子需求的排擠效應。
2. 美債避險功能仍在，中天期債券風險報酬相對均衡：10 月 29 日科技股下跌時，10 年期

美債殖利率由約 5.05%回落至約 4.99%，顯示美債仍具一定避險效果。惟 Fed 鷹派立場
及油價高檔使短天期公債易受升息預期影響 ， 超長天期公債則面臨財政赤字與期限溢價壓
力，中天期公債風險報酬相對均衡。投資級債中期維持偏正向；高收益債則須留意 AI 相
關發行人自由現金流轉負及利差擴大風險。

### Page 2

Investment Thesis - NYPCB 
NYPCB manufactures and markets printed circuit boards (PCBs) and integrated circuit 
(IC) substrates (including ABF and BT substrates). We are Buy rated on NYPCB 
considering the company’s high exposure to BT substrates and non-LTA ABF substrate 
(70%+ of total revenue), where we expect GM/OPM to increase significantly in the 
coming quarters (from breakeven in OPM in 1H25 to 26/42% in 2026/27E) due to the 
favorable substrate pricing outlook. Despite the company’s low exposure to high-end 
ABF substrate industry (<20% of total revenue in 2025), we believe the expanding 
demand from ASIC AI server and high-end switch IC demand will drive the company’s 
high-end ABF revenue exposure to 40%+ by 2027E, suggesting better GM/OPM outlook 
in the long term. In 2H25-2H26, we believe NYPCB will continue to benefit from stronger 
ABF/BT pricing and should be a key beneficiary of the pricing uptrend considering the 
company’s key focus on BT and non-LTA ABF substrate industries. Moreover, in the long 
term (after 2026), we expect the ABF industry to return to supply tightness, and NYPCB’s 
expanding high-end exposure will lead to a long-term earnings growth outlook (148% 
2026-28E earnings CAGR). As a result, we see solid upside potential for NYPCB’s 
earnings and stock performance.  
Price Target Risks and Methodology - NYPCB 
Valuation methodology: Our 12-month target price of NT$2,700 for NYPCB is based 
on 21x 2028E P/E, which is +1.5x STDV higher than the past upcycle average P/E (13.3x).  
Key downside risks: (1) Slower-than-expected PC demand recovery, (2) 
Slower-than-expected ABF substrates pricing upgrading outlook, and (3) 
Slower-than-expected NYPCB new high-end capacity qualification progress.

Zhen Ding Technology
Valuation
We set a DCF-based TP of NT$750. We use a DCF-based valuation to reflect ZDT's ability to generate stable cash flows. Key
assumptions of our DCF model include a risk-free rate of 5.2% (10-year government bond rate), a market risk premium of 7%,
and an equity beta of 0.89 (1-year weekly share price), deriving a WACC of 10.7%. Our model assumes high-single-digit long-

term growth after 2028E and a 21.6% EBIT margin from 2028E with a gradual annual increase. Our TP is equivalent to 27x/17x
2027E/28E EPS and 4.5x/3.8x 2027E/28E BVPS.
Risks
Key downside risks that could cause the stock to trade below our target price include: 1) lower-than-expected
iPhone/Mac/iPad end-demand; 2) lower content value for new smartphone models or pricing pressure; 3) ZDT losing market
share from its FPC/HDI peers; 4) a slower demand in the substrate industry; 5) failure to pass the qualifications of major AI

GPU or ASIC customers.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

### Page 1

All Ring

Accelerated advanced packaging and CPO 
ramp optionality at attractive valuation

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 1,750 TWD | Price: 1,025 TWD

2H26 strength sustains on 2.5D packaging expansion 
3Q26 sales were +41% QoQ above Street estimates on solid CoWoS and FOCoS capacity 
expansion across its foundry and OSAT customers, backed by the accelerated capex from 
back-end industry to accommodate stronger cloud AI chipset demand outlook into ‘27. 
With mix shifted toward margin-diluted automation tools, 3Q26 GMs were -630 bps QoQ 
to 48.5% (vs. BofAe 54.1%) though OpMs held resilient QoQ at 32.9% on operating 
leverage, taking 3Q26 EPS to NT$8.50 (vs. BofAe/Street NT$10.74/NT$9.34.). For 4Q26, 
we estimate sales -9% QoQ due to CoWoS revenue recognition timing partially offset by 
sustained strength in FOCoS. We expect GMs in 4Q26 to recover to its long-term guidance 
of 50-55% range (BofAe 53.7%) on better product mix.   
Diverse 2.5D/3D packaging and CPO ramp lifting ‘27-28 
All Ring’s sales are on track to +88% YoY in ‘26 and could see continued strength into ‘27 
(BofAe +55% YoY), mainly led by 80-90k WPM CoWoS addition from TSMC to 200-210k 
WPM by 4Q27 along with ASE’s accelerated FOCoS capacity expansion and CoWoS full 
process ramp for outsourced business. With superior throughput and yield stability vs. its 
peers, we expect its CPO optical coupling equipment shipment to reach 150 units in ‘27, 
backed by a solid commitment from Nvidia/SPIL to increase CPO assembly capacity by 15x 
(see our report: Implication from Nvidia GTC: back-end capacity constrained; CPO 
accelerates). Broader equipment share and content gains across diversifying advanced 
packaging technologies, including FOPLP, SoIC, CoPoS and US back-end solutions, 
alongside the ramp-up of CPO equipment should drive All Ring’s advanced packaging 
revenue +60% YoY in 2027, lifting GM/OpM to 54.2%/33.3% from 52%/32.2% in 2026.  
Reiterate Buy on decent EPS upcycle with +34% CAGR 
We maintain our 2027/28 EPS unchanged at NT$49.34/54.67, our Buy rating on All Ring 
and PO of NT$1,750 still based on 36x 2027E P/E (vs. its 8-41x range). We believe 
decent sales/earnings CAGR of +30%/+34% over '26-'28E supported by solid advanced 
packaging capacity build and smooth CPO ramp should warrant high valuation.

Company Sector 
Semiconductor Capital Equipment

Company Description 
Founded in 1996, All Ring is a Taiwan-based semiconductor 
equipment supplier with core technologies spanning 
dispensing, attachment, AOI, optical coupling, and precision 
automation. Building on its initial expertise in passive 
component manufacturing equipment, the company has 
gradually expanded into the back-end semiconductor 
packaging market and established long-term partnerships 
with leading foundry and OSATs customers.

Investment Rationale 
We have a Buy rating on All Ring, in view of its solid market 
position in the semiconductor advanced packaging 
equipment. We believe its long-standing relationships with 
leading foundry and OSATs, coupled with strong technology 
development capabilities, position the company well to 
capture the secular uptrend in AI-driven advanced 
packaging capacity expansion and rising equipment 
content.

### Page 4

Price objective basis & risk 
All Ring (XNQNF) 
Our PO of NT$1,750 is based on 36x 2027E P/E, positioned at the upper half of its 
historical trading range of 8-41x. We believe P/E based valuation is appropriate given All 
Ring's robust top-line visibility, stable profitability profile, and structural earnings 
improvement on decent exposure to AI-driven advanced packaging upcycle. The 
valuation multiple is underpinned by 
1) 34%/37% CAGR in sales and earnings over 2026-28E and 2) sustained ROE in the 30-
40% range

Downside risks are: 
1) slowdown in advanced packaging capacity expansion and 2) mis-execution on 
equipment development

義隆(2458.TT) 
產業：電子–半導體 
深受 NB 出貨趨緩影響，短期營運受壓抑 
 投資評價與建議 
NB客⼾拉貨⼒道趨弱將壓抑整體營運動能 ， 無⼈機相關產品為主要成⻑來源 ，
2027年營運展望保守，給予30倍PER*2027年EPS評價。 
 重點評論分析 
4Q26 NB產業承壓，營運同步面臨逆風：NB客⼾拉貨保守，雖有短/急單，但
延續性恐不佳 ， 僅有無⼈機相關產品表現較佳 ， 推估營收16億元 ， QoQ-27.9% ，
且仍持續面臨供應鏈成本壓⼒，推估毛利率43.9%，EPS 0.51元。 
短期NB缺料問題難解，2027年營運成⻑動能恐受限：展望2027年，IDC預估
PC出貨量YoY-4.1% ， 而義隆營收約80%以上與NB相關 ， 因此短期公司營運亦
同處於逆風，主要動能來自非NB產品的無⼈機：公司與中研院合作，利用自身
演算法優勢，推出Mini LED local dimming(區域調光)與ISP產品，1H26出貨
以ISP為主，ASP約5~6美元，7月已開始出貨AI Box，ASP約300~500美元，
已通過雷虎測試並開始出貨，2H26 ASP明顯提升，為2026年最佳成⻑動能，
也是推升AI應用占比提升主要動能，2026年AI營收YoY+40%，也是未來公司
重心，且公司具備MIT優勢，將積極爭取國防訂單，未來具成⻑潛⼒。整體而
言，短期NB客⼾拉貨恐保守，主要仰賴無⼈機等非NB產品推升，營運成⻑較
受限。 
 投資風險 
NB需求進⼀步下滑或非NB貢獻不如預期。

### Page 2

2

| 營運概況  
3Q26營運受NB影響而趨緩，獲利低於預期：3Q26營收22.2億元，QoQ-30.8%，主因1H26 NB客⼾提前拉貨及
缺料造成拉貨⼒道下滑，毛利率44.7%，主因產品組合轉差及供應鏈成本提升(包含模組的被動元件及PCB漲價)，
費用因嚴控成本而下滑至6.96億元，業外主要來自金融評價損失，EPS 0.58元，優於預期。 
4Q26 NB產業承壓，營運同步面臨逆風：NB客⼾面臨CPU、記憶體缺料問題，拉貨保守，雖有短/急單，但延續
性恐不佳，僅有無⼈機相關產品表現較佳，推估營收16億元，QoQ-27.9%，且仍持續面臨供應鏈成本壓⼒，推估
毛利率43.9%，EPS 0.51元。 
短期NB缺料問題難解 ， 2027年營運成⻑動能恐受限 ：受記憶體與CPU缺貨持續壓抑NB出貨成⻑性 ， 以及NB漲價 、
AI排擠商用NB需求等因素，雖1H26提前拉貨推升公司營運仍佳，然自3Q26起NB客⼾需求趨緩，使得公司營運逐
季下滑，展望2027年，IDC預估PC出貨量YoY-4.1%，而義隆營收約80%以上與NB相關，因此短期公司營運亦同
處於逆風，主要動能來自1)Haptic滲透率持續提升，因Haptic ASP達15美元，明顯優於傳統clickpad 2美元，將
帶動觸控板ASP提升，2026年Haptic營收貢獻約3.5~4億元(2025年約0.6億元)，2027年也將持續提升；2)ADAS
來自電動巴士，2026年出貨double，已有第六位客⼾開始小量出貨，2027年持續成⻑，主要來自政府電動巴士
補助所推升，政府最近也補助電動物流車，2027年確實落地後，公司將有電動物流車的成⻑機會，量也比電動巴
士多很多；3)無⼈機：公司與中研院合作，利用自身演算法優勢，推出Mini LED local dimming(區域調光)與ISP
產品，1H26出貨以ISP為主，ASP約5~6美元，7月已開始出貨AI Box，ASP約300~500美元，已通過雷虎測試並
開始出貨，目前僅作前端光學模組，2026年底將推出避障功能，四鏡頭方案約500美元，十二鏡頭方案將達1,000
美元 ， 2H26 ASP明顯提升 ， 為2026年最佳成⻑動能 ， 也是推升AI應用占比提升主要動能 ， 2026年AI營收YoY+40% ，
也是未來公司重心，且公司具備MIT優勢，將積極爭取國防訂單，未來具成⻑潛⼒。整體而言，短期NB客⼾拉貨
恐保守，主要仰賴無⼈機等非NB產品推升，營運成⻑較受限。 
2027年營運動能受限，維持中立評等：NB客⼾拉貨⼒道趨弱將壓抑整體營運動能，無⼈機相關產品為主要成⻑來
源，2027年營運展望保守，維持中立評等，給予30倍PER*2027年EPS評價。

## Document 202: 37亿美元“数据中心发电设备预付款”不要了！“燃气轮机最积极买家”软银看到了什么？

Source PDF: all report pdf/37亿美元“数据中心发电设备预付款”不要了！“燃气轮机最积极买家”软银看到了什么？.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-11
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

软银 2026 财年第⼆季度录得 71 亿美元净亏损，核⼼原因是为美国数据中⼼锁定发电设备的
95 亿美元预付款中，有 37 亿美元被直接计提减值 —— 其中约 29 亿美元因不可退还已打⽔
漂。 CFO 承认，软银曾在项⽬⽅案未定前就抢先下单，⽬前正对采购策略进⾏全⾯重新评
估。 “ 燃⽓轮机最激进的买家，就这样把押⾦给核销了。 ”
摘要：
软银集团 2026 财年第⼆季度（截⾄ 9 ⽉）净亏损 1.12 万亿⽇元（约 71 亿美元），为近两年来⾸次
季度亏损。
市场此前预期的是 1290 亿⽇元的利润。结果和预期⽅向完全相反。
亏损的直接导⽕索，是⼀笔 37 亿美元的资产减值 —— 据彭博报道，这笔钱原本是为锁定 AI 数据
中⼼发电设备⽽提前⽀付的预付款，如今⼤部分已⽆法追回。
软银 CFO Yoshimitsu Goto 在业绩发布会上承认，公司此前 “ 抢在⻓期规划敲定之前，就急于锁
定所需设备 ” ，如今正对整体采购策略进⾏ “ 全⾯重新评估 ” 。
GLJ Research 创始⼈在研报中说：“ 燃⽓轮机排队名单上最激进的买家，就这样把押⾦给核销

了。 ”
消息传出后，软银股价周三早盘在东京市场暴跌逾 10% 。 GE Vernova 、 Bloom Energy 等电⼒设
备供应商股价随之下挫，市场对 AI 基础设施投资热度能否持续的疑虑明显升温。
钱是怎么没的？
软银通过旗下⼦公司 Silver Bands 7 （ SB7 ） —— 由软银与愿景基⾦ 2 共同持股 —— 在过去⼀年
半⾥，持续向设备制造商⽀付预付款，⽬的是锁定制造产能和发电设备。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

问题在于：这些钱是在项⽬⽅案敲定之前就付出去的。截⾄今年 9 ⽉底，累计预付⾦额已达 1.5 万
亿⽇元（约 95 亿美元）。
随后，项⽬需求发⽣变化， SB7 启动 “ 设备采购策略修订 ” ，⽽这笔 37 亿美元账单随之分两部分落
地：
4570 亿⽇元（约 28.5 亿美元）：为预订产能和设备所⽀付的预付款，因合同条款不可退还，直
接损失；
1400 亿⽇元（约 8.8 亿美元）：⽤于修改和终⽌合同的结算款，资⾦尚未全部付出，将从本季
度起陆续⽀付。
ZeroHedge 分析称： “ 软银为 AI 电⼒设备预付的每⼀美元中，有 30 美分已经蒸发，⽽结算⽀票还
没全部寄出去。 ”
剩余约 66 亿美元的设备款项，已被划拨⾄俄亥俄州园区及其他项⽬继续使⽤。
作为安慰， SB7“ 可能 ” 在未来⼗年内获得最⾼ 21 亿美元的设备采购返利 —— 但软银⾃⼰的审计师
对此并不买账，未将其计⼊任何资产。 Goto 在财报中也仅表⽰，公司 “ 可获得最⾼ 21 亿美元的返
利 ” ，措辞留有余地。

CFO ：项⽬没停，但要 “ 全⾯重新评估 ”
软银 CFO 后藤芳光在财报电话会议上表⽰，公司此前为了锁定 AI 数据中⼼所需设备， “ 有时甚⾄
在⻓期规划尚未敲定之前就抢先下单 ” 。
他同时强调，俄亥俄州数据中⼼项⽬并未叫停，⽬前正在进⾏ “ 全⾯重新评估 ” ，包括对采购⽅式
的审查，但未透露具体细节。
对于美国中期选举结果（⺠主党候选⼈以反对数据中⼼为竞选纲领获胜）可能带来的影响，后
藤芳光表⽰：需要密切关注其对 AI 情绪的冲击。
“ 最积极买家 ” 的退出，市场在担⼼什么？
GLJ Research 创始⼈在研报中直接点名：“ 燃⽓轮机排队名单上最激进的买家，就这样把押⾦给
核销了。软银是⼀个⽼练的买家，能接触到全球每⼀家贷款机构。如果连它都在不清楚⾃⼰需
要什么的情况下就下单，我们怀疑它并⾮孤例。 ”
这句话的⾔下之意是：如果软银都在重新评估，其他 AI 基础设施买家是否也在做同样的事？
不过，从设备供给端看，情况并没有那么悲观。 GE Vernova 截⾄第三季度末已有 122 吉⽡的燃

⽓设备在合同中，并将年末⽬标上调⾄⾄少 130 吉⽡。分析师预计其每股收益将从今年的 15.68
美元增⻓⾄ 2028 年的 37.13 美元。
GE Vernova ⽅⾯也表⽰，订单押⾦和产能预留费均不予退还。这意味着，如果软银放弃部分产
能槽位， GE Vernova 将保留押⾦，并可将槽位重新出售。
更深的隐忧：利息负担在攀升
37 亿美元的减值是⼀次性损失，但软银⾯临的财务压⼒并不只是这⼀笔。

### Page 3

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

1 条评论
软银上半财年（截⾄ 9 ⽉）集团整体利息⽀出达 6760 亿⽇元，同⽐翻倍以上。预计截⾄ 2028 年 3
⽉的财年，年度利息⽀出将超过 1 万亿⽇元（约 60 亿美元），⾼级贷款还款合计 1.4 万亿⽇元（约
90 亿美元）。
新光资产管理策略师泽村⼀树表⽰： “ 可以将 AI 数据中⼼计划调整带来的损失视为暂时性的。更
令⼈担忧的是财务成本的上升。如果投资收益和持仓价值⽆法从现在起稳步增⻓，利息负担可
能压缩盈利，并加剧外界对软银财务健康状况的担忧。 ”
⽬前，软银的贷款价值⽐（ LTV ）为 15.9% ，低于其设定的 25% 警戒线。截⾄ 9 ⽉底流动性超过
320 亿美元，是此前的两倍以上。
但信⽤分析师 Sharon Chen 指出，扣除未来两年到期债券和最后⼀笔 100 亿美元 OpenAI 投资

后，超额流动性约为 100 亿美元，软银⼤概率将在 2027 年进⾏新⼀轮融资。
孙正义的 AI 押注：还在加码
即便如此，软银的 AI 投资步伐并未停下。
孙正义已累计向 AI 相关硬件和应⽤投⼊数百亿美元，包括对 OpenAI 近 650 亿美元的投资，以及
以 31 亿美元收购数据中⼼运营商 DigitalBridge 集团。
与此同时，软银股价已较 6 ⽉历史⾼点累计跌去逾 50% ，年初⾄今下跌约 20% 。
AI 估值本⾝也在承压。据知情⼈⼠透露， Anthropic 的估值已从此前宣传的 2 万亿美元降⾄约 1.1
万亿美元； OpenAI 最新⼀轮融资约 110 亿美元，估值 1.25 万亿美元，低于最初寻求的 1.4 万亿美
元⽬标。

(26.10.29)

방위산업

◼ 투자의견 매수, 목표주가 1,640,000원 유지 
◼ 컨센서스 및 추정치 대비 매출, 영업이익 하회 
◼ 4Q26 및 27년 실적 추정치 상향

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 1,640,000원 유지 
4Q26E-2Q27E 지상방산 및 항공우주 EBITDA에 Target 12MF EV/EBITDA 25.0x 
적용, 상장 자회사 가치 SOTP 통해 목표주가 산출. 3Q26 실적 아쉬우나 투자포
인트에는 변함 없어 멀티플은 변경 없이 적용. 27년 실적 추정치 상향에 따라 본
업 가치 상승, 26년 스페인 자주포 양산 및 미국 자주포 시제 사업자로 선정되며 
현지화 전략이 유효했음을 증명. 3Q26 실적에 대한 우려를 소화하며 글로벌 피어
들과의 멀티플 격차 축소를 전망함

컨센서스 및 추정치 대비 매출, 영업이익 하회 
3Q26 연결 기준 매출액 6.1조원(-6.0% YoY), 영업이익 4,520억원(-46.2% YoY, 
OPM 7.4%)으로 컨센서스인 매출액 7.4조원 및 영업이익 1.1조원을 크게 하회
하는 실적 시현. 하회의 주요 요인으로는 3Q26 필리조선소 적자폭 확대, 지상
방산 부문 실적이 부진한 영향. 지상방산 부문의 경우 매출액 1.2조원(-41.0% 
YoY), 영업이익 220억원(-96.2% YoY, OPM 1.7%)을 시현했는데, 폴란드 관련 
매출이 부재했던 1Q26 영업이익률 16.9%보다도 낮은 실적을 보임. 이는 국내
외 사업 모두에서 악재가 겹쳐 발생했던 것으로 보이는데 국내 사업에서는 마
진율이 낮은 사업이 매출로 인식되었고 수출 사업에서는 폴란드 매출 부재 뿐 
아니라 이집트, 호주 K9 자주포 매출 인식 또한 예상 대비 부진해 고정비 레버
리지 효과로 3Q26 영업이익률이 크게 훼손된 것으로 추정. 이외 대전사업장 복
구에 따른 비용, 기타 사업 충당금 등 총 500억원 수준의 일회성 비용이 발생했
으며 영업외손익에서는 원화 강세에 따른 환손실이 주로 반영되었음

4Q26 및 27년 실적 추정치 상향 
1Q26에서 3Q26까지 이뤄지지 않았던 폴란드 EC2 K9 자주포 30문 인도는 
4Q26에 집중될 것으로 보이며 폴란드 EC1 천무 또한 기존 가이던스였던 26년 
천무 40대에서 60대로 인도 대수 상향이 이뤄짐. 이에 따라 4Q26 지상방산 영
업이익률은 22.8%로 회복을 전망. 27년에는 폴란드 K9 자주포 및 천무 인도 대
수 증가로 26년 수출 비중 58.0%에서 77.2%로 증가, 이에 따라 영업이익률 또
한 매출 믹스 개선에 따라 19.8%에서 28.0%로 개선을 전망

## Document 253: [207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 3Q26 Pre_ 파업과 환율 부담에도 컨센 부합삼성바이오로직스(207940) 3Q26 Pre_ 파업과 환율 부담에도 컨센 부_c581d324

Source PDF: all report pdf/[207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 3Q26 Pre_ 파업과 환율 부담에도 컨센 부합삼성바이오로직스(207940) 3Q26 Pre_ 파업과 환율 부담에도 컨센 부_c581d324.pdf

### Page 1

삼성바이오로직스 (207940/KS) 
3Q26 Pre: 파업과 환율 부담에도 컨센 부합 
2026-10-06

제약/바이오. 이선경 / seonkyoung.lee@sks.co.kr / 3773-9089

매수(유지) 
목표주가: 2,000,000 원(유지) 
현재주가: 1,354,000 원 
상승여력: 47.7%

Signal: 파업과 환율 부담에도 컨센 부합 전망  
Key: 5 공장 매출 본격화, 미국 공장 매출 기여에 따른 성장 기대   
Step: 단기 불확실성보다는 중장기적 성장 방향성에 주목

3Q26 Pre: 파업과 환율 부담에도 성장지속, 컨센 부합 전망   
26년 3분기 연결기준 매출액은 1조 3,741억원(+9.3%YoY, +4.0%QoQ), 영업이
익은 6,098억원(-3.7%YoY, +4.0%QoQ, OPM 44.4%)으로 컨센서스 매출액 1
조 3,450억원과 영업이익 6,063억원에 부합하는 실적을 전망한다.  
5월 파업 영향으로 약 20개 배치 생산분(약 1,500억원)에 대한 원가 손실이 인식되
고 3분기 평균 환율이 전분기 대비 약 5% 하락했음에도 불구하고, 3분기부터 매출로 
인식되는 5공장 PPQ 배치 및 미국 공장의 매출 기여로 지속적인 성장이 예상된다. 약 
1,500억원 규모의 생산 차질분은 고객사와 일정 조율을 통해 출하될 예정으로 이에 
따른 4분기 매출 성장을 기대해 볼 수 있다.   
신규 수주 회복과 모달리티 확장에 주목 
시장에서 우려했던 신규 수주는 관세 이슈로 고객사의 컨택이 감소하면서 올해 저조했
으나 4 월 Section 232 결과 발표 이후 논의가 회복 단계에 있어 연내 추가 신규 수주 
가능성은 열려 있다.  
항체 시장의 경쟁심화와 차세대 모달리티 확장에 대응하기 위해 결정한 폴리펩타이드
의 인수는 연내 마무리될 예정이다. 폴리펩타이드의 2028 년 목표 EBITDA 마진은 
25%로 동사 대비 낮은 상황이나, 펩타이드 기반 CDMO 시장 성장에 따른 마진 개선
도 가능하다. 현재 임단협, 환율, 미국 공장 및 폴리펩타이드 인수에 따른 회계 영향 등 
불확실성이 큰 상황이나, 수주 회복과 5 공장 매출 인식 본격화, 미국 공장 및 멀티 모
달리티 확장 등 중장기 성장 방향성에 주목할 필요가 있다.

본 보고서에 언급된 종목의 경우 당사 조사분석담당자는 본인의 담당종목을 보유하고 있지 않습니다.

당사는 자료공표일 현재 해당기업과 관련하여 특별한 이해 관계가 없습니다.

## Document 269: [373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) ESS 수주 모멘텀 이어진다LG에너지솔루션(373220) ESS 수주 모멘텀 이어진다ESS 수주 모멘텀 이어진다 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) ESS 수주 모멘텀 이어진다LG에너지솔루션(373220) ESS 수주 모멘텀 이어진다ESS 수주 모멘텀 이어진다 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-11-04
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

2026.11.04   
LG에너지솔루션 
(373220)

ESS 수주 모멘텀 이어진다 Company Brief

3Q26 실적, 원-달러 환율 하락에도 시장 기대치 대폭 상회 
동사의 3Q26 실적은 매출 9.6조원(+59% YoY, + 28% QoQ), 영업이익 7,560억원 
(+26% YoY, +568% QoQ)으로 원/달러 환율 하락에도 불구하고 시장기대치(매출 
8.8조원, 영업이익 3,217억원)를 크게 상회했다. 당사는 Stellantis와의 캐나다 
합작관계 종료에 따른 보상금 등을 포함해 약 4,250억원의 일회성 이익이 반영된 
것으로 추정한다. 이를 제외한 영업이익은 약 3,310억원으로, 시장기대치에 대체로 
부합한 것으로 판단된다. AMPC는 미국 ESS 생산량 증가와 GM 얼티엄셀즈 재가동에 
힘입어 전분기 대비 약 73% 증가한 4,169억원을 기록했다. 사업부문별로는 ESS의 
실적 개선이 두드러졌다. 상반기 배터리 패키징 병목으로 이연됐던 물량 출하가 
집중되면서 ESS 출하량은 전분기 대비 60% 가량 증가한 것으로 추정된다. 자동차 
전지도 유럽향 물량 회복과 GM 얼티엄셀즈 재가동으로 매출이 전분기 대비 약 
33%로 증가했으며, 고정비 부담 완화에 따라 적자폭도 뚜렷하게 축소된 것으로 
판단된다. 소형전지는 주요 고객사인 Tesla의 판매 호조에 따른 출하 증가로 매출 
회복과 수익성을 뒷받침한 것으로 보인다. 
4Q26 ESS 중심의 수익성 개선과 수주 모멘텀 지속될 전망 
미국 ESS 시장은 신재생 에너지 확대와 AI 데이터센터의 전력 수요 증가에 힘입어 
빠르게 성장하고 있다. 특히 정책 환경도 비PFE 공급망을 확보한 업체에 유리하게 
작용하고 있다. 동사는 3Q26 실적 컨퍼런스 콜에서 ESS 수주잔고가 약 
160GWh이며, 2028 년까지 캐파의 대부분에 대응하는 물량을 확보했다고 밝혔다. 
또한 현재 다수의 고객사들과 2028년 이후 공급 물량을 협의하고 있어 연말까지 
추가적인 수주 모멘텀이 이어질 것으로 예상된다. 이를 바탕으로 4Q26에도 동사의 
본업 실적은 ESS 중심의 개선세를 이어갈 전망이다. 전분기에 반영된 대규모 일회성 
이익의 기저효과로 영업이익은 감소하겠지만, 이를 제외하면 출하량 증가와 가동률 
상승에 따른 점진적인 수익성 개선이 예상된다. 수익성 중심의 사업 효율화를 위해 
소형 IT용 파우치 사업을 축소하고 있다는 점도 중장기 손익 개선에 긍정적이다. 이를 
반영한 4Q26 매출과 영업이익은 각각 약 10.5조원(+63% YoY, +9% QoQ), 
4,150억원(흑자전환 YoY, -45% QoQ)으로 추정된다. 
매수 투자의견과 목표주가 유지 
동사에 대한 매수 투자의견과 목표주가를 유지한다. 북미 ESS 수요 증가와 공급망 
재편에 따른 신규 수주 확대가 중장기 실적 성장을 뒷받침할 전망이다. 특히 ESS용 
생산라인 전환과 가동률 상승이 본격화되면 매출 증가에 더해 고정비 부담 완화와 
AMPC 수혜 확대가 맞물리면서 수익성도 개선될 것으로 예상된다. ESS 의 역할이 
신재생 에너지 연계용 저장장치를 넘어 AI 데이터센터의 안정적인 전력 공급을 
지원하는 핵심 인프라로 확대되고 있다는 점에도 주목할 필요가 있다. 이에 따라 
동사의 밸류에이션 역시 기존 EV 중심의 평가에서 벗어나 전력 인프라 산업의 
성장성을 반영하는 방향으로 재평가되어야 한다. 업종 내 실적 개선 가시성과 정책 
수혜가 뚜렷하고, 4Q26 신규 수주에 따른 주가 상승 가능성도 높다는 점에서 동사에 
대한 비중 확대 전략이 유효하다는 판단이다. 
Buy (Maintain)

## Document 283: AI数据中心建设降温也不必担心？美国进口热潮短期难退，真正冲击或到2029年

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI数据中心建设降温也不必担心？美国进口热潮短期难退，真正冲击或到2029年.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-11
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

数据中⼼项⽬从确定投资到开⼯建设平均需 4.8 年，从开⼯到投⼊运营还需 2.6 年，服务器
通常要等到建筑结构和机架安装完成后才能进场。对中国台湾、韩国和墨西哥等依赖美国
数据中⼼供应链需求的贸易伙伴⽽⾔，建设周期的需求惯性⾄少为未来⼀两年的出⼝提供
了缓冲。
摘要：
美国 AI 数据中⼼建设正⾯临政治阻⼒，但即使新项⽬开⼯放缓，也未必会迅速终结美国的进⼝热
潮。
彭博经济研究指出，数据中⼼建设周期较⻓，服务器等核⼼硬件通常在项⽬后期才采购， 2025
年和 2026 年启动的项⽬仍将⽀撑未来两年的进⼝需求。真正明显的进⼝冲击，可能要到 2029 年
才会显现。
数据中⼼商品成为美国进⼝增⻓引擎
特朗普第⼆任期以来，关税政策已将美国平均有效关税税率从 2.3% 推⾼⾄约 11% 。但数据中⼼
建设热潮推动相关商品进⼝持续增⻓，抵消了其他商品进⼝的疲软。
彭博经济研究数据显⽰， 2026 年以来，其他商品进⼝持续低迷，但数据中⼼相关商品进⼝激
增，使美国⽉度进⼝额仍⾼于 2024 年同期。其中，计算机硬件尤其是服务器，近⼏个⽉约占数

据中⼼相关商品进⼝的 70% ，成为主要增⻓动⼒。
美国从台湾、墨西哥等地进⼝的计算机硬件显著增加。此外，相关进⼝还包括联⽹设备、铜线
和钢制机架等。许多数据中⼼相关商品，尤其是计算机硬件，还获得了特朗普新关税的豁免。
新项⽬放缓，短期进⼝冲击有限
政治阻⼒可能⾸先影响数据中⼼建设的早期阶段。本⽉美国中期选举后的政策变化，以及部分
州推出的新限制措施，可能令尚处于规划阶段或即将开⼯的项⽬放缓。
但美国数据中⼼建设规模仍处于⾼位。彭博新能源财经（ BNEF ）数据显⽰，美国 2025 年新增数
据中⼼容量接近 10 吉⽡，⽽ 2022 年不⾜ 3 吉⽡。此前 BNEF 预计，到 2028 年美国每年新增容量可
能超过 15 吉⽡。即使建设速度低于预期，仍将明显⾼于过去⼏年的⽔平。
此外，项⽬还可能从限制较严格的地区转移⾄政策相对宽松的地区，进⼀步减轻对美国整体建
设规模和进⼝需求的冲击。
服务器采购滞后，进⼝压⼒或延⾄ 2029 年
更重要的是，数据中⼼建设周期决定了进⼝需求不会随新项⽬放缓⽽⽴即降温。

BNEF 统计显⽰，从确定投资到开⼯建设平均需要 4.8 年，从开⼯到投⼊运营还需要 2.6 年。服务
器则通常要等到建筑结构和机架安装完成后才能进场，属于建设后期采购的设备。
据亚⻢逊介绍，数据中⼼⽀出可能在服务器安装前约两年就已启动。这意味着， 2025 年和 2026
年开⼯的项⽬，将成为 2027 年和 2028 年进⼝增⻓的重要⽀撑。即使新开⼯项⽬⼤幅减少，相关
影响也可能要到 2029 年才会实质性体现。

### Page 2

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

部分在建项⽬甚⾄已经提前订购服务器，只是尚未交货。供应受限也促使客⼾更早锁定硬件订
单，进⼀步延缓进⼝对新项⽬放缓的反应。
AI 泡沫破裂将带来更猛烈冲击
彭博经济研究认为，短期内，美国数据中⼼进⼝需求的下⾏⻛险相对有限。若新项⽬减少，电
⽓设备、冷却设备和建筑材料等建设早期所需商品可能率先受到影响，但这些商品在本轮进⼝
热潮中的占⽐较⼩。
真正更⼤的压⼒，将在原本应当为受影响项⽬采购服务器的时间点显现。
不过，如果 AI 泡沫破裂，导致连在建项⽬也被搁置，进⼝需求受到的冲击就可能提前到来，且程
度更加猛烈。
总体⽽⾔，美国数据中⼼建设⾯临的政治阻⼒，并不意味着进⼝热潮即将退去。对于中国台
湾、韩国和墨西哥等依赖美国数据中⼼供应链需求的贸易伙伴⽽⾔，建设周期带来的需求惯

性，⾄少为未来⼀两年的出⼝提供了缓冲。

## Document 375: How the blue wave could reshape US energy and data centres

Source PDF: all report pdf/How the blue wave could reshape US energy and data centres.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-05
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Energy aﬀordability shaped the midterms. A divided
government will now face pressure to shield consumers from
rising data centre power costs while speeding up new energy
supply. Onshoring and trade protections will continue, despite
higher near-term costs, as energy security remains a priority
Coco Zhang
After the midterm elections, the government will need to minimise data
centre-related costs for consumers, bring new power supply online faster and

protect supply chains. Pictured: Data centre in Ohio
Exit polls from the US midterm elections highlighted a key concern
shaping voters’ decisions: energy aﬀordability. Nearly 90% of
voters said the cost of living was a crucial or major factor in their
vote for Congress, with many pointing to gasoline and utility costs.
Meanwhile, as the AI data centre boom strains power supply and
infrastructure, 56% said the issue was a crucial or major factor in

their vote.
With Democrats now set to control both the House and Senate,
what does this mean for US energy and data centre policy?

### Page 2

Newly elected lawmakers will face pressure to address these
concerns, requiring the Democratic Congress and Republican White
House to ﬁnd common ground on shielding consumers from data
centre-related costs and accelerating new power supply, including
through permitting reform. Onshoring and supply chain protections
are also likely to continue. While they can raise near-term costs,
they remain important to US energy security. Developments in all
three areas could have signiﬁcant implications for businesses.

That said, Congress needs to strike a balance when drafting
legislation aimed at achieving these goals. More aggressive
measures could face a veto from President Trump.
Strengthened data centre regulation ahead,
but the devil is in the detail
The data centre industry is facing heightened local opposition.
Recent polls suggest that around one in seven Americans is
concerned about or opposes data centre development in their
neighbourhoods.
In response to this issue, there is likely to be tighter data centre

regulation. State-level policy will be more important to watch, as
action is politically and practically easier at this level. The most
likely approach is targeted regulation, much of which is already
emerging. This includes data centre-related taxes, reduced tax
incentives, environmental standards, and requirements for
developers to bear relevant cost increases. Outright moratoriums
remain unlikely to become mainstream. Where they are introduced,

they are more likely to be temporary, giving states time to develop
a data centre policy framework.
At the federal level, the key unanswered question is how much
authority should remain with the states and whether federal
legislation should mandate state regulations or simply provide
guidance. Republicans generally favour letting states lead, while
Democrats tend to support a stronger federal role.
We think sweeping data centre legislation is unlikely because of

President Trump’s veto power. Federal action is more likely to focus
Your privacy is important

### Page 3

on aﬀordability by protecting electricity customers from higher
costs and formalising data centres' responsibility to help fund grid
and energy infrastructure upgrades. Such measures could be added
to broader legislation, such as must-pass spending bills needed to
avoid a government shutdown, or a bipartisan package like
permitting reform.
More broadly on AI, we expect the Democratic Party to increase
scrutiny of technology companies, as discussed here, while utilising

the next two years to prepare an AI governance framework for 2028
and beyond.
However, tighter AI and data centre regulation is unlikely to
fundamentally alter the industry’s growth trajectory. AI’s
importance to US economic growth and competition with China will
remain powerful drivers of expansion.
Business implications
State-level policy would more directly aﬀect project siting,
which is important for data centre companies to watch.
Where state rules are tighter, project development could slow.

However, a more restrictive regulatory environment could
also increase the value of data centres already approved or
under construction, easing some of the industry’s capital
constraints. Higher valuations could also encourage
developers to be more selective and expand at a more
measured pace, ultimately improving the quality of the
project pipeline.
Developers should also treat community engagement as a
strategic investment. Recent surveys show that while only

20-30% of Americans support local data centre construction,
support can rise to 50-70% when there are accompanying
community investments, such as in public infrastructure
improvements or reductions in residents' costs. More
developers are now signing binding community beneﬁt
agreements as part of project development.

Enabling faster power generation through
permitting reform
Both parties support timely expansion of electricity supply to meet

rising demand from AI and manufacturing reshoring. They also
generally agree that energy abundance can help reduce cost
pressures. While Democrats and Republicans diﬀer on the preferred
energy mix, there is still common ground. Technologies likely to
retain bipartisan support include nuclear, geothermal, carbon
capture, and biofuels.
However, the greatest potential for policy action may lie not in
speciﬁc technologies, but in permitting reform.
Today, many energy projects can spend over ﬁve years navigating

the federal permitting process. Around $240bn to $280bn of
infrastructure investment across the eight sectors below enters the

With both parties recognising the need for reform, the Senate
introduced the Bipartisan American Aﬀordability and Jobs Act
(BAAJA) in September 2026. Key permitting proposals include:

Speeding up approvals for energy projects
Providing greater certainty and consistency across
administrations
Making it easier to permit power transmission lines
Despite debate over its environmental impact, there is broad
agreement that the BAAJA would meaningfully improve the
permitting process. The key question is whether it can become law
under the current Congress. The Senate is expected to vote on 9
November, after which the bill would still need approval from the

House and President Trump. Democrats may want to delay the bill
and seek changes once they regain control of Congress next year,
but doing so could increase the risk of a presidential veto. This
creates an incentive to reach a deal sooner rather than later.

### Page 6

Even if the BAAJA does not pass under the current Congress,
permitting reform is likely to remain a bipartisan theme. The
growing need to expand power supply and infrastructure more
quickly will keep pressure on future Congresses to streamline
permitting.
Business implications
For energy companies – both renewables and fossil fuels –
the BAAJA could signiﬁcantly shorten permitting timelines. If
enacted, it would give project developers greater certainty,

reducing the risk of litigation and permit cancellations. This
could further expand project siting options for developers.
Nevertheless, in the very near term, projects may need to
retain ﬂexibility around their permitting strategy while the
new policy framework is developed and implemented.
Expect more protectionism and reshoring
efforts against China dominance
From clean energy technologies to the critical minerals that
underpin much of the US economy, China controls a large share of

global production capacity. Consequently, reducing reliance on
China will remain a US policy priority. Such eﬀorts would increase
rather than reduce project costs in the near term. But the long-term
beneﬁts of stronger domestic manufacturing and more secure
supply chains could outweigh those costs.
Global supply chain capacity distribution
across various sectors

### Page 7

Source: ING Research, BloombergNEF, International Energy Agency
This creates two key risks. First, heavy reliance on China leaves US
industries vulnerable to geopolitical tensions. We have already seen
this with rare earths, where Chinese export restrictions contributed
to disruptions for US auto manufacturers. Second, China’s supply
chain dominance can put US manufacturers at a competitive
disadvantage.
Building more secure energy and natural resource supply chains

has long attracted bipartisan support in the US. The Trump
administration has supported domestic rare earth production
through funding, purchase agreements, and equity investments. In
solar, China-focused trade and tax credit restrictions are also
supporting the expansion of domestic manufacturing.
We expect this focus to continue. The executive branch could
pursue further protective measures and is unlikely to face major
opposition from Congress. Congress could also authorise additional

funding to strengthen rare earth supply chains.
Furthermore, a Democratic-controlled Congress could push to
extend clean manufacturing tax credits as part of broader
negotiations with President Trump. Given the administration’s focus
on countering China and expanding US manufacturing, an
extension could emerge as a compromise in a broader legislative
package under an optimistic scenario. All these eﬀorts would also
extend into future administrations.

Business implications
For US companies:
Clean energy companies and other industries that rely on
rare earths, including semiconductors, AI, defence, and

electronics, should have enough policy certainty to build
long-term supplier relationships outside China. In the near
term, however, diversiﬁcation could raise supply chain costs
as alternative sources may be more expensive. Solar could
face particular cost pressures, as some tariﬀs on supply
chain equipment also apply to non-Chinese suppliers.
For overseas companies:
US eﬀorts to build supply chains outside China are creating
opportunities for manufacturers elsewhere. The US is

supporting rare earth production in Australia and Brazil, while
solar and battery suppliers in countries such as South Korea
and Japan could beneﬁt from stronger demand. This could
also encourage more manufacturing investment in the US.
Meanwhile, European clean energy developers could face
greater competition for non-Chinese supplies. While the EU
has not imposed US-style tariﬀs, it is tightening restrictions
on Chinese equipment through funding, procurement,

auction, and subsidy rules. This could intensify global
competition for non-Chinese suppliers and push costs higher.

## Document 425: September CPI_ Inflation accelerating in the run up to Christmas (2026-11-12)

Source PDF: all report pdf/September CPI_ Inflation accelerating in the run up to Christmas (2026-11-12).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 18
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#inflation #australia #rba #economicbulletinsaustralia #inflation
September CPI: Inflation accelerating in the run up to Christmas
By Justin Smirk & Neha Sharma
16:08 October 28 2026 Share
September Monthly CPI: Headline 0.8%mth/4.3%yr, Trimmed Mean 0.3.%mth/3.6%yr.
Market services ex volatile 1.6%mth/4.0%yr. September Quarter CPI: Headline
1.3%qtr/3.9%yr; Trimmed Mean 1.0%qtr/3.7%yr.
The CPI rose 0.8% in the September month, stronger than Westpac’s 0.6% estimate and the

market’s expectation of 0.7%. The annual pace picked up from 4.0%yr to 4.3%yr, The seasonally
adjusted CPI rose 0.6% in September highlighting some positive seasonality in the month.

### Page 2

Key factors driving the headline monthly result were: automotive fuels (9.4%mth); domestic holiday
travel & accommodation (8.0%mth); international holiday travel & accommodation (7.2%mth); and
property rates & charges (5.5%mth). There were modest offsets from: women’s garments (–
3.1%mth); household textiles (–6.6%mth); and accessories (–4.1%mth).
The Trimmed Mean (TM) gained 0.3% in September, spot on both Westpac and market expectations
with the annual pace lifting from 3.5%yr (revised from 3.6%yr) to 3.6%yr. The six-month annualised

pace is now 4.0%yr the fastest in the short history of this series.
Headline CPI lifted 1.3% in the September quarter compared to an expectation of 1.3% by Westpac
and 1.2% by the market. This saw the annual pace hold at 3.9%yr.
The RBA’s key underlying inflation measure, the quarterly TM, lifted 1.0% vs an expectation of
0.9% by Westpac and market estimate of 1.0%. The annual pace lifted from 3.6%yr to 3.7%yr
with the six-month annualised pace accelerating to 3.7%yr from 3.4%yr.

Overall, the estimates come in broadly as expected so we see minimal risk to our current December
quarter forecast of 1.3%qtr for the headline CPI. Softer housing inflation does present a very modest
downside risk to our 0.9%qtr for the TM but there are offsetting gains elsewhere.
The September CPI update revealed underlying inflationary pressures continuing even while a
moderation in housing inflation (dwellings in particular) is unfolding slightly faster than we had

anticipated. As such, the near-term risk for inflation remains to the upside. The RBA will remain alert
to these near-term inflationary risks and as so, we hold to the view that the RBA will raise rates again
at the November meeting.

### Page 3

Month of September CPI
The monthly CPI rose 0.8%mth in September, above both our expectation of a 0.6%mth increase and
the market forecast of 0.7%mth. This lifted the annual pace by 0.3ppts to 4.3%yr. On a seasonally
adjusted basis, the CPI increased 0.6%mth.
The key contributors to the monthly increase were: automotive fuel (9.4%mth, +0.3ppts); and holiday
travel & accommodation (7.6%mth, +0.5ppts), alongside a smaller contribution from the annual reset in

property rates (5.5%mth, +0.1ppt). There was no single major offset. Instead, several components
across household furnishings and clothing & footwear recorded modest declines, together subtracting a
little over 0.1ppt from the monthly result.
The monthly trimmed mean (TM) rose 0.3%mth, in line with both our and the market’s expectations.
The annual pace edged up to 3.6%yr, although the August outcome was revised down to 3.5%yr.
Underlying inflation has remained broadly stuck in the 3.5–3.6%yr range since May. On a 3m/3m basis,

the monthly TM was 1.1%.

### Page 4

Market services excluding volatile items and holiday travel were flat in the month, with annual inflation
easing to 3.8%yr, its softest pace since November 2025. On a seasonally adjusted basis, monthly
inflation moderated across most components in this measure, with telecommunications services, rents
and other financial services the key exceptions. However, all but two components are still recording
annual price growth above 3%, suggesting that disinflation across market services remains slow and

relatively narrow. The persistence of these underlying price pressures is likely to remain a concern for
the RBA.

### Page 5

Housing inflation was notably softer in the month, rising 0.3%mth to be 5.2% higher over the year.
Rents increased 0.2%mth, slightly below our expectation for a 0.3%mth rise. The biannual indexation
of Commonwealth Rent Assistance occurred late in the month, weighing on the September outcome,
with the remainder of the impact expected to flow through in October. The ABS noted that rents would
have risen 0.3%mth in the absence of the measure, while on a seasonally adjusted basis rents

increased 0.4%mth.
New dwelling inflation was flat in the month, the softest reading since May 2025. Monthly inflation has
now slowed in each successive month since May 2026, suggesting homebuilders’ pricing power is
becoming increasingly constrained. The ABS noted that some project home builders raised base prices
this month only to be offset by some builders increasing discounts and promotional offers in response
to slowing demand. The annual pace eased to 4.7%yr. Dwelling repair costs were also a downside

surprise, falling 0.1%mth, their first decline since February 2025, although prices remain 3.4% higher
over the year. Some of this softness was offset by a firmer-than-expected increase in property rates
and charges, which rose 5.6%mth.
Recently, we have also focused on audio, visual and computing given the potential for spillovers from
strong global demand for semiconductors and other computing resources associated with the AI build
out. Prices for both equipment and services came in weaker than August, with equipment prices falling

1.1%mth, while services rose 0.3%mth. Despite this, annual price growth remains relatively high for
these categories, running at 4.6% and 4.9% respectively. Some of this pressure might also be showing

### Page 6

through in gaming, toys and hobbies, which is running at 5.2%mth/15.5%yr.
Overall, the breadth of inflation moderated for a second consecutive month. The share of the basket
running above 3%yr fell from 40% to 35%, meanwhile the share below 2%yr lifted from 27% to 32%.
Our replication of the San Francisco Fed’s inflation shock momentum served as a good guide to the
quarterly TM. With the full monthly reads in for the September quarter in, this measure does signal
some persistence/upside pressures for the quarterly TM going into the coming quarters. It is worth
cautioning that this measure will be impacted as the ABS refines its seasonal adjustment process for
the monthly indexes.

Compared to our expectations, the key surprises were:
Upside surprises
Downside surprises
Fruit & vegetables was the key upside surprise, 0.6%mth against our expectations for 3.7%mth fall.
Holiday travel & accommodation also surprised to the upside, following a run of downside surprises
since early this year. It rose 7.6%mth against our expectations for a 4.4%mth lift. 
Automotive fuel rose 9.4%mth against expectations of an 8.3%mth lift.

### Page 7

September Quarter CPI
Clothing & footwear was the key downside surprise with prices falling 2.7%mth against our
expectations for a 0.2%mth fall. The misses were broad-based: garments (–2.3%mth vs –2.0%mth);
footwear (–2.5%mth vs 1.8%mth); and accessories & clothing services (–4.1%mth vs 2.8%mth).
Household textiles declined 6.6%mth against our expectations for a 3.2%mth fall. Furniture &
furnishings rose by less-than-expected 0.4%mth vs 1.6%mth. Household appliances, utensils and

tools also came in softer, –3.2%mth vs –2.4%mth.
Dairy prices fell 1.2%mth against our expectations for a 0.1%mth lift.

### Page 8

The September quarter CPI printed spot on our expectation of 1.3%. The key factor driving this
increase was the slightly stronger surge in auto fuel (7.8%, +0.27ppt) and holiday travel &
accommodation (2.7%qtr, +0.16ppt) being offset by a slightly softer than expected increase in housing
(1.1%qtr, +0.24ppt) with rents and dwellings both increasing 1.0% in the quarter and utilities lifting
1.2%. Food was also slightly stronger than expected (0.7%qtr, +0.12ppt) and while not significant to the
overall result it is interesting to track the ongoing rise in motor vehicle prices (0.3%qtr) despite a very
competitive market with Chinese manufacturers rapidly gaining market share over legacy brands.

It is worth noting that food was stronger than we expected (0.7% vs 0.5%) with a robust 1.7% increase
in restaurant meals and a solid 1.2% increase in take away & fast foods outweighing the –0.4% decline
in fresh fruit & vegetables and meat & seafoods. Overall, market services ex volatile prices increased a
very solid 1.2%qtr but with the annual pace easing to 3.2%yr from 3.36%yr.
Outside of housing the other main downside surprise in the quarter was the –0.6% fall in clothing &
footwear (+0.3% expected) with prices falling across just about all categories of garments, footwear
and accessories.
September Quarter Trimmed Mean

### Page 9

Recall that the TM is calculated from seasonally adjust consumption expenditure classes so any
numbers quoted here may not be the same as the original estimates quoted from the CPI. The TM gain
of 1.0% in the quarter was slightly stronger than we had expected even though dwellings (which have a
weight of almost 11% in the TM) was a touch softer at 1.0% compared to the 1.1% we had pencilled in.
Rents (1.0%, 10% weight) also made a solid contribution as did restaurant meals (1.7%qr, 6% weight),

take away & fast food (1.2%, 4% weight), other financial service (1.0%, 6% weight). Domestic travel
was in the TM (1.0%, 4% weight) as was medical & hospital service (1.0%, weight 5%). The range of
the TM was from 0.0% (motor vehicles) to 1.7% (beer).
What does this mean for our December quarter forecast and into 2027?
We are working though the monthly results to see what changes they may imply for individual
component inflationary momentum. Our preliminary analysis suggests that the softer trend in dwellings

is not enough, on its own, to change our Q4 CPI forecast of 1.3%. It does, however, suggest that if all
else held equal (which is not the case as there is likely to be some offsetting stronger gains elsewhere)
the moderation in dwelling inflation presents a very modest downside risk our Q4 TM estimate of 0.9%.
We are still working the numbers and will publish an updated inflation profile as soon as we can. In
addition, the overall momentum in underlying inflationary pressures suggest the downside risk to the

TM from dwellings is more likely to appear in 2027 than in the last quarter of 2026.

## Document 447: Top of Mind_ Higher bond yields_ how concerning_

Source PDF: all report pdf/Top of Mind_ Higher bond yields_ how concerning_.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-10-13
Pages: 31
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The Goldman Sachs Group, Inc.
Global bond yields have risen to multi-decade highs, yet growth and markets have
remained remarkably resilient. But with some pressures beginning to emerge (e.g. in
European sovereign bond markets), can this resilience last, or will more vulnerabilities
be revealed? We ask the IMF’s Tobias Adrian, Moody’s Analytics’ Mark Zandi, and GS
economists. Adrian is relatively sanguine about the economy’s resilience, though
notes downside risks, while GS economists expect only a modest growth drag from

higher rates, but a more consequential one if yields remain near current or higher
levels. Zandi is generally more concerned, describing the US economy as vulnerable
rather than resilient, and sees one-in-three odds of the economy going off the rails.
Meanwhile, GS strategists and analysts are not especially concerned about banks or
markets more broadly, but warn that further increases in yields or rate volatility could
test risk assets. Key to watch: whether the increasingly interconnected and leveraged

AI investment boom can continue to withstand higher rates.
Tobias Adrian, Special Advisor and former Director of Monetary and Capital Markets, IMF
“The global economy has demonstrated considerable resilience in the face of multiple severe shocks, and I would not be
surprised if it continues to shrug off higher yields, provided they do not keep rising sharply in a disorderly fashion. At the same
time, I do worry about downside risks.”
Mark Zandi, Chief Economist, Moody’s Analytics

We provide a brief snapshot on the most important economies for the global markets

### Page 3

El 
Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research 3 
Top of Mind Issue 152

Recent acceleration in the rise in global bond yields to multi-
decade highs has begun to raise questions about the 
implications for growth and markets. Both have remained 
remarkably resilient so far, with US growth in particular 
holding relatively firm and the S&P 500 and other equity 
markets near new all-time highs. But with pressure 
beginning to emerge—most notably in European sovereign 
bond markets—and yields looking unlikely to retreat soon, 
can that resilience last, or will more vulnerabilities be revealed? 
GS Head of US Rates Strategy William Marshall first lays out 
how we arrived at higher rates, underscoring the striking 
breadth of the rise, which he attributes mainly to 
fundamentals—strong growth, inflation risks, hawkish policy 
shifts, the ongoing investment cycle, and long-run fiscal 
risks—but notes that technical factors likely amplified the 
recent move. And while he ultimately expects yields to fall 
(with YE 10-year US Treasury yields of 4.75%/4.4% in 
2026/2027, respectively) as markets become more confident 
in easing inflation, he thinks elevated long-term yields should 
have some staying power, and sees continued upside risk 
from energy price and fiscal pressures.  
So, just how much of a threat do higher rates pose to the 
macro outlook? For answers, we first turn to Tobias Adrian, 
Special Advisor to the Managing Director and former 
Financial Counsellor and Director of the Monetary and Capital 
Markets Department at the IMF, Mark Zandi, Chief 
Economist of Moody’s Analytics, and GS US economists. 
Adrian sees a global economy that has so far responded to 
the yield shock much as it has to the many other major 
shocks of the past seven years: resiliently. He notes that the 
rise in yields has not significantly tightened financial 
conditions or, more importantly, disrupted market functioning 
or forced damaging deleveraging. He believes that resilience 
could persist as long as the move in yields doesn’t accelerate 
or become more disorderly. But he is concerned about 
vulnerabilities in the AI and technology complex, sovereign 
bond markets, and EMs—not to mention cyber risks—all of 
which leaves him “vigilant but cautiously optimistic.” 
GS US economists also characterize the US economy as 
resilient. Their expectation that the Fed will deliver just one 
more rate hike this year before cutting next year—consistent 
with Marshall’s expectation of lower Treasury yields—leads 
Pierfrancesco Mei to expect only a modest 0.2pp growth 
drag in 2027 from higher rates. But he finds that the drag 
could rise to a more consequential 0.5pp if yields remain near 
current levels (see pg. 9 for GS global economists Joseph 
Briggs’ and Johan Allen’s rules of thumb for the impact of 
higher yields on rate-sensitive areas across major DMs.) 
David Mericle and Mei also worry about the implications for 
the already-concerning US fiscal trajectory, which they 
believe is unlikely to trigger a classic debt crisis but is too risky 
to ignore.     
Zandi, for his part, is generally more concerned. While his 
baseline outlook is relatively sanguine, he describes the US 
economy as vulnerable rather than resilient and views the 
“distribution of possible outcomes as very flat and heavily 
skewed toward darker tail scenarios.” He sees the clearest 
softness in the labor market, with weak hiring and falling real 
wage growth for many workers weighing on consumer 
spending. And he takes little comfort from US growth 
continuing to run around 2%—roughly its potential rate—
noting that AI accounts for roughly one-third of that growth, 
leaving the non-AI economy growing at only around 1-1.5%, 
which he calls “a pretty punk economy.” Zandi also worries 
that the US fiscal situation is “as bad as I have ever seen it” 
and that higher rates are already inflicting damage on real 
estate markets and squeezing existing debtors. As these 
stresses mount, he sees elevated risk of something breaking 
in the financial system. All told, he puts the odds of the 
current vulnerabilities pushing the economy completely off 
the rails at around one-in-three.  
With banks historically at the center of the rate shocks and 
economic crises that Zandi is worried about, we then explore 
how much risk banks pose today. Adrian and GS Head of the 
Financials Equity Group Richard Ramsden both see little 
cause for alarm, explaining that higher rates should boost 
banks’ net interest income and revenues given their asset-
sensitive balance sheets. Ramsden also doesn’t expect a 
significant deterioration in credit quality absent a broader 
economic downturn and says that most banks should have 
excess capital even after accounting for rate-driven portfolio 
losses.   
But even if banks can weather higher rates, what about 
markets more broadly? GS strategists and analysts are not 
especially concerned, with Chief US Equity Strategist Ben 
Snider arguing that current rate levels shouldn’t prevent 
continued strong US earnings growth and the S&P 500 from 
climbing higher, Chief Credit Strategist Amanda Lynam 
viewing the headwind to corporate credit from higher rates 
as manageable amid the solid growth backdrop, and US 
Financials Analyst Alexander Blostein expecting the recent 
slowdown in private market activity to rebound once rates 
stabilize. GS multi-asset strategists Christian Mueller-
Glissmann and Andrea Ferrario add that higher yields have 
strengthened the strategic case for bonds in a multi-asset 
portfolio, though strong earnings continue to favor equities 
for now. But they all warn that if rate volatility remains 
elevated, and especially if yields continue to rise from here, 
risk assets could face mounting pressures.  
All that said, with the AI boom powering the economy and 
markets, everything may come down to whether higher rates 
will derail it. Snider views this as unlikely given the strong 
balance sheets of the firms investing in AI and the expected 
profitability of these investments, with Lynam also seeing a 
high bar to derail AI-related debt issuance. Adrian also 
maintains a positive baseline for the AI economy but is 
concerned about the circularity of investment, increased 
leverage, and the sustainability of revenue growth in the AI 
complex. And Zandi again seems the most worried, warning 
that “AI could become the part of the economy that breaks” 
if rates remain at current levels for much longer.  
Allison Nathan, Editor
